# M2 independent reviews

Commit reviews of the M2 task branches through `tools/agent_task.py --role reviewer`, each in a fresh native session separate from the main development session. Raw transcripts stay in the ignored `.hoh-dev/attempts/`; this file keeps the verdicts, the findings and how each was handled.

## M2-T01 review round 1 (fresh Codex session)

- Agent: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0, session `01a1a4e0-30ed-7fe3-be6c-0a655ed7bdec`, 651 s; attempt record sha256 `63047b0d6d096fb87651c0b481b8115f30ab704f027698ad1a3ba9b8124be1d7`, stdout sha256 `020b773b95cbfe3c81999a2539e63a6109b44869618db409e5e08b9bdc1d467b`
- Scope: `ca07412..33e0d29` (hoh-core::persistence, the AC-062-05 scanner, docs), candidate `CAND-e239f1db…`, 646 manifest files matched at the end; cargo test (72 + 1 ignored helper), `scripts/product/static.sh` and the bundle (343, 1 skip: no Pi packages in the reviewer's copy) PASS; the mutation script re-run in a copy (14 killed, 2 survived, as recorded); probes for COMMIT-time FK rollback, writer panic (reply channel closed), draining queued jobs at shutdown, a caller timing out after its job was queued (it may still commit), and SQLITE_BUSY at COMMIT in rollback-journal mode; injected lock and mapping faults; minimal programs in a copy that write through rusqlite outside the Persistence module; no model call. Also re-reviewed the M1-T02 follow-ups `c10b2ba..ca07412` (crates/hoh-agents): **no findings** — the doc comment is accurate, both timing fixes passed 40/40 runs, the escape test fails on the old script with an injected `setsid` delay and passes on the new one, and the R-01 test still catches a removed cancel latch.
- Verdict: **FAIL** (4 high, 2 medium).

| ID | Severity | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| M2T01R-01 | high | mod.rs:293 — the location check looked at the existing ancestor of the path as written while the database was created through it: a `state.db` symlink to an NFS directory, or `local/missing/../../network/state.db`, were accepted and the database created on the network mount (injected mountinfo). | Fixed: `location::resolve` makes the path absolute and resolves every symbolic link (also a dangling one for the last component, whose target is where the file would be created; at most 40 links) and `..` after missing directories (lexically, as they cannot be links); the check, `create_dir_all`, the self-test and the open all use the resolved path, and `Store::path()` reports it. Test `links_and_parent_components_are_resolved_before_the_location_check` (dangling file link, linked directory, `missing/../..`; nothing created on the network side; a link to local storage is followed) red on `33e0d29` (the store opened through the link); unit test `resolve_follows_links_and_dangling_links` (also a link loop). |
| M2T01R-02 | high | selftest.rs:108 — two connections in one process share SQLite's in-process lock and wal-index state; with all `F_SETLK(W)` made no-ops or `MAP_SHARED` turned into `MAP_PRIVATE` the self-test still passed. | Fixed: the self-test's shared-memory and locking steps now run against a second **process** (`ProbeCommand`; in the product the `hoh` binary's hidden `__wal-probe` mode, which runs `selftest::probe_main`). The probe must see the rows in the WAL, then a row we commit after it opened (a private or per-process wal-index shows a stale count), and we must see its commit; each side must be refused BEGIN IMMEDIATE while the other holds it. A probe that cannot start or does not answer within its timeout fails the step. Tests: `a_probe_without_working_locks_or_shared_memory_fails_the_test` (a real `nolock=1` probe connection, a probe that claims locks it does not take, one that ignores our lock but takes its own — added when the mutation run showed the first lock check otherwise untested — and a probe answering like a private wal-index), `a_probe_that_cannot_start_or_does_not_answer_fails_the_test`, and hoh-app `the_hoh_binary_is_the_wal_probe` (the built binary serves the probe). The old single-process self-test had no second process to fault, so these tests have no red run on it; the reviewer's injections are the evidence for the old behaviour. |
| M2T01R-03 | high | persistence_scan.py:95 — undecoded per-literal regexes missed `format!("UPDATE {table} SET …")`, `concat!("INSERT ", "INTO …")`, a continuation inside a keyword and `DELETE\x20FROM`; a program in hoh-app that wrote through `prepare(…).raw_execute()` scanned PASS. | Fixed at the root: the scan now rests on what the compiler enforces — (1) dependencies by package identity from `cargo metadata`: no workspace member but hoh-core reaches a SQLite package through normal or build dependencies other than via hoh-core (dev-dependencies are listed, not hits); (2) inside hoh-core the crate names under which it sees SQLite occur only in `persistence/`; (3) no plain-`pub` item of `persistence/` mentions a SQLite crate or connection type — to make that true, `pragmas(conn)`, `verify_pragmas` and `Reader::with` became crate-private (`with` test-only). The SQL literal check stays as defence in depth with Rust decoding (escapes, continuations without an inserted space) and comma-separated literals joined as `concat!` does. Bundle tests cover each reproduction; the scanner of `33e0d29` missed them all on the same inputs. A copy of `33e0d29` with a renamed rusqlite in hoh-app, scanned with real `cargo metadata`, reports the dependency and the three formerly public SQLite-typed functions. |
| M2T01R-04 | high | persistence_scan.py:106 — dependencies were matched by key name only: `[dependencies.rusqlite] workspace = true` and `db = { package = "rusqlite" }` passed, as did a web `{"db": "npm:better-sqlite3@12"}`. | Fixed with R-03 (package identity from `cargo metadata`, which resolves renames and workspace inheritance; a stale lockfile makes `cargo metadata --locked` fail and the scan FAIL). Web manifests and lockfiles are checked by name and by version spec (`npm:` aliases). Tests `test_sqlite_dependencies_outside_hoh_core_are_hits_by_package_identity` (renamed, transitive through another crate, dev-dependency allowed) and `test_web_sqlite_dependencies_and_imports_are_hits`. |
| M2T01R-05 | medium | mod.rs:264 — `user_version` was checked after the script's own COMMIT, so a failing version check left committed DDL behind (and a retry then failed). | Fixed: the runner owns the transaction — each migration runs in one BEGIN IMMEDIATE transaction, its statements executed one by one with the script's `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`END` skipped (the contract schema has them), and it commits only if `user_version` then equals the migration's version. `a_failing_migration_is_rolled_back` now asserts the schema and `user_version` after the silent-version case and that the fixed migration applies afterwards; red on `33e0d29` (`extra` table left). |
| M2T01R-06 | medium | persistence_scan.py:22 — any `.execute()`/`.transaction()` call was a hit, so an unrelated `Command.execute()` failed the scan. | Fixed: the method-name rule is gone; with (1)–(3) of R-03 no code outside the Persistence module can reach a SQLite connection. `test_the_persistence_module_may_use_sqlite_and_others_may_name_things_freely` includes such calls. |

Mutation evidence (raw): `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t01-mutations/mutations.json` with `mutate.py` beside it, run on the clean fix commit: every mutant is killed except the two equivalent ones from the first run (dropping the explicit `foreign_keys` and `synchronous` settings: the bundled SQLite already defaults to foreign keys on and synchronous FULL, and `verify_pragmas` — whose mutant is killed — refuses a connection where they differ).

## M2-T01 review round 2 (focused, fresh Codex session)

- Agent: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0, session `01a1a4fb-fa61-7481-bef2-380f2d6967e8`, 707 s; attempt record sha256 `1caa77e44368c5674c766177cee64b45a268514623cd999305d39df0c2492de5`, stdout sha256 `c3305d18a9e42a5ced11d3f43aef0331a8103e9042c3522bd69c6a197fb8a8a7`
- Scope: fix delta `33e0d29..9eabc78`, candidate `CAND-5b67c041…`, 648 manifest files matched before and after; round-1 reproductions re-run; injected no-op `F_SETLK(W)` and `MAP_PRIVATE` against the two-process self-test (both now fail it and clean up); compiled-and-executed bypass programs in copies (type alias re-exported through hoh-agents, `extern crate` alias, a hoh-core build script, a proc macro); a path swap during the self-test; migration comments and triggers; a deferred-FK COMMIT failure; a probe wrapper whose child outlives it; real `cargo metadata` online, offline with and without a cache, and with a stale lockfile (the latter two fail the scan); cargo test (77 + 2 ignored helpers), static.sh and the bundle (346, 1 skip) PASS; mutate.py in a copy (18 killed, 2 survived as recorded; the two survivors confirmed equivalent for the bundled build: writer and reader read foreign_keys=1 and synchronous=2, also after switching to WAL); no model call
- Verdict: **FAIL** (2 high, 3 medium, 1 low). R-02 and R-06 RESOLVED; R-01, R-03, R-04, R-05 PARTIAL through the findings below.

| ID | Severity | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| M2T01R-07 | high | persistence_scan.py:136, 204 — the public-API and crate-name checks went by literal names in unexpanded src/ files: a `Connection` alias returned publicly and re-exported by hoh-agents, `pub(crate) extern crate rusqlite as r2db` used elsewhere in hoh-core, a hoh-core build script and a proc macro each wrote SQLite outside the Persistence module while the scan passed. | Fixed: the SQLite names are closed over the aliases hoh-core's sources define (`extern crate X as Y`, `use … as Y` in a use naming one, `type Y = …` naming one) and may occur only in `persistence/`, checked in the code of **every** workspace member's product sources — `src/**/*.rs` and build scripts (also custom-build targets from `cargo metadata`) — and, for proc-macro crates, in their string literals too; the public-API rule uses the same names; hoh-core may not have a SQLite package as a build-dependency. Bundle test `test_aliases_build_scripts_and_proc_macros_are_followed` covers the four reproductions. The script's docstring and docs/22 now state the limit plainly: it checks source text against ordinary and accidental violations; identifiers assembled at compile time are left to review. |
| M2T01R-08 | high | mod.rs:336–357 — between the location check and the open (during the self-test) the checked `local/state.db` could be replaced by a link to an NFS directory; the store opened and created state.db/-wal/-shm there. | Fixed: after the check the directory must still resolve to itself, every open (store, readers, self-test database) uses `SQLITE_OPEN_NOFOLLOW` — SQLite then refuses a link anywhere in the path before creating anything — and the device (`st_dev` → mountinfo `major:minor`) of the directory and, after opening, of the database file are checked against the network list. Replacing a component with a real directory from another file system needs a mount (root): rename cannot cross file systems. Test `a_path_swapped_after_the_check_is_refused` (the file or its directory replaced by a link to the network mount right after the check; nothing opened or created there) and unit test `devices_are_matched_against_the_mount_table`. |
| M2T01R-09 | medium | mod.rs:297 — transaction control was recognised after whitespace only: `-- c\nCOMMIT` and `/* c */ END` committed early, `-- c\nBEGIN` failed as nested. | Fixed: the leading keyword is taken after any `--` and `/* */` comments; in addition, any executed statement that leaves the runner's transaction (autocommit again) fails the migration. Cases added to `a_failing_migration_is_rolled_back` (both comment forms with a missing version, and a script with `ROLLBACK`); `migration_scripts_keep_trigger_bodies_and_roll_back_a_failed_commit` checks that a trigger's `BEGIN … END` body is kept. |
| M2T01R-10 | medium | persistence_scan.py:234 — only the `packages` keys of a lockfile were looked at, not a package's `name` or its nested dependencies (`npm:better-sqlite3@12`). | Fixed: lockfiles are searched through — every key and string value. Test `test_web_lockfiles_are_searched_through` (v3 lockfile with a transitive alias and a package object's name). |
| M2T01R-11 | medium | selftest.rs:166 — on timeout only the direct probe process was killed; a child it started survived and could keep the pipe. | Fixed: the probe runs in its own process group, which is killed (SIGKILL) before the leader is reaped; its members holding the stdout pipe die with it, ending the reader thread. Test `a_probe_that_hangs_is_killed_with_everything_it_started`. |
| M2T01R-12 | low | mod.rs:274 — a failing COMMIT of a migration left the transaction open (the current caller drops the connection). | Fixed: any failure of a migration, COMMIT included, rolls back when a transaction is still open; checked in `migration_scripts_keep_trigger_bodies_and_roll_back_a_failed_commit` (deferred foreign key violated at COMMIT: error, connection back in autocommit, no schema change). |

Mutation evidence after the round-2 fixes (`.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t01-mutations/mutations.json`, 26 mutants including NOFOLLOW, the directory re-check, comment-aware transaction control, the transaction-ending check, ROLLBACK after a failed COMMIT and the probe group kill; run on clean `39950e2`): 24 killed, the same 2 equivalent survivors. The run first exposed three gaps, fixed in `39950e2`: the commented-`COMMIT` case was masked by the commented-`BEGIN` case (now separate cases with their own expected error), the directory re-check was masked by NOFOLLOW (the test now asserts its specific error), and without the group kill reaping the probe could hang (dropping a probe now also kills the leader and closes its stdin; a hanging test counts as a killed mutant).

## M2-T01 review round 3 (focused, fresh Codex session)

- Agent: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0, session `01a1a51e-b1a6-73d1-bcbf-e6b9ca208a2b`, 763 s; attempt record sha256 `5b1f7bb213071c589c102ef455979f69ef522be0263e3e55c6f2dca7e281ec68`, stdout sha256 `fba3e40dfc0c5ae0e9d348c2d63e083e963da6cb6936926f13275146dd8cded2`
- Scope: fix delta `9eabc78..d45de0e` (16 files), candidate `CAND-b9b437a9…`, 648 manifest files matched before and after; round-2 reproductions compiled and executed in copies (all four scanner bypasses now refused; path swaps of the file and its directory refused with nothing created on the network side; commented transaction control; the transitive lockfile alias; the hanging probe with a child; the deferred-FK COMMIT failure); symlinked project/tmp/home ancestors and later readers; device matching for btrfs/overlay/tmpfs fixtures and real /tmp and /dev/shm; comments containing keywords, CTE writes, trigger bodies and EXPLAIN in migrations; the scan on the real repository (no false positive); cargo test (81 + 2 ignored helpers), static.sh and the bundle (348, 1 skip) PASS; mutate.py in a copy (24 killed, the 2 equivalents survived); no model call
- Verdict: **FAIL** (2 high, 1 medium). R-08, R-10, R-11, R-12 and round-1 R-01, R-04 RESOLVED; R-07, R-09 and round-1 R-03, R-05 PARTIAL through the findings below.

| ID | Severity | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| M2T01R-13 | high | persistence_scan.py:236 — the alias closure followed `use … as` only: `use rusqlite::Connection; type R3Db = Connection;` with a public factory returning `R3Db`, re-exported through hoh-agents, let hoh-app write through `execute_batch` while static.sh passed. | Fixed by making the boundary Rust's module visibility and checking it, instead of chasing name patterns: nothing in `persistence/` that is visible outside it (items and fields that are `pub`, `pub(crate)`, `pub(in …)` outside persistence, or `pub(super)` in mod.rs) may mention a SQLite name, where a file's SQLite names are the crate names, **everything its `use` statements import from them** (renamed or not), and every alias or re-export of one defined anywhere in persistence; a `type` alias of a SQLite type (associated types such as `Deref::Target` included), a trait impl whose header names one, and `#[macro_export]` in persistence are hits too. To meet the rule, the crate-visible helpers that took a `Connection` became private to the module (`sqlite_error`, `pragmas`, `verify_pragmas`, `migrate`, `MIGRATIONS`, `Reader::with`) and the writer's and self-test's internals `pub(super)`. Bundle test `test_unrenamed_imports_aliases_and_trait_impls_are_followed` (the reviewer's case, an alias made in one persistence file and returned from another, Deref and AsRef impls); the round-2 scanner passed the reviewer's case, the new one fails it. |
| M2T01R-14 | high | persistence_scan.py:212 — files were found by `crates/*/src/**/*.rs` and build scripts only, so a module compiled through `#[path = "../database_ops.rs"]` writing SQLite was not scanned. | Fixed: product sources are all `.rs` files of the member directories except tests/, benches/ and examples/, and `#[path = …]` and `include!` are not allowed in product sources (no compiled file can come from elsewhere). Bundle test `test_path_attributes_and_include_are_hits_and_stray_files_are_scanned`; the round-2 scanner passed the reviewer's case. |
| M2T01R-15 | medium | mod.rs:297 — an empty statement or a byte-order mark before `COMMIT`/`END` hid it: `CREATE TABLE a(x); ; COMMIT;` committed before the post-statement check failed the migration. | Fixed: transaction control is decided before execution with SQLite's own classification — transaction control is read-only (`sqlite3_stmt_readonly`), so a read-only statement runs only if its keyword (after whitespace, BOM, empty statements and comments) is a known read (`SELECT`, `WITH`, `VALUES`, `EXPLAIN`, `PRAGMA`); the script's `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`END` are skipped and any other read-only statement fails the migration unexecuted. Cases added to `a_failing_migration_is_rolled_back` (`; COMMIT`, `/* c */ ; END`, BOM before both statements); `ROLLBACK` in a script is now refused before it runs. |

Mutation evidence after the round-3 fixes (28 mutants, run on clean `9c7e6ff`): 25 killed. Survivors: the two equivalents above, and the post-execution check that a statement ended the runner's transaction — now unreachable, because SQLite classifies all transaction control as read-only and the gate refuses or skips it before execution; it stays as defence in depth should that classification ever differ.

## M2-T01 review round 4 (focused, fresh Codex session)

- Agent: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0, session `01a1a538-f462-7340-9a15-6be3da8be0a2`, 773 s; attempt record sha256 `2c2e17256ce74064892100fe29ec5bfa4d69df2e0b24cb11adabf09fa8c9e062`, stdout sha256 `c8f787bc1bf18a5158fbcae5068daa42f9faf4d98a8ef4208e18df28bb5ce833`
- Scope: fix delta `d45de0e..519defc` with the whole change `ca07412..519defc`, candidate `CAND-aec725b0…`, 648 manifest files matched before and after; every scanner bypass compiled and executed in copies against a database initialised with the project schema (real `cargo metadata --locked`, SQL passed at run time, no identifiers assembled at compile time); migration classification probes (PRAGMA writes, ANALYZE, REINDEX with and without indexes, VACUUM, ATTACH/DETACH, SAVEPOINT/RELEASE/ROLLBACK, CTE writes); cargo test (81 + 2 ignored helpers), static.sh and the bundle (350, 1 skip) PASS; mutate.py in a copy (25 killed, 3 survived as recorded); no model call
- Verdict: **FAIL** (5 high, 1 medium). R-15, R-09 and round-1 R-05 RESOLVED; R-13, R-14, R-07 and round-1 R-03 PARTIAL through the findings below. The reviewer confirmed the two equivalent mutants (foreign_keys=1 and synchronous=2 by default in this build) and that "all transaction control is read-only" is false (`BEGIN IMMEDIATE/EXCLUSIVE` are not), which is R-20.

| ID | Severity | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| M2T01R-16 | high | persistence_scan.py:159 — multi-line function signatures were cut to their first line (taken for fields); a generic factory returning `(Connection, Option<T>)`, a crate-visible `FnOnce(&Connection)` callback, a public trait method and an enum payload each handed a connection out while the scan passed. | Fixed by moving the enforcement to the compiler (rounds 1–4 each found another gap in reasoning about Rust visibility from text): `clippy.toml` `disallowed-methods` lists every rusqlite method that opens a database or executes SQL (45 paths for the enabled features), only `persistence/` carries `#![allow(clippy::disallowed_methods)]`, and the static check runs clippy with `-D warnings` and fails on an entry that does not resolve. Clippy resolves each call with type information, so a call outside persistence fails however the connection was obtained. `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t01-clippy-boundary/probe.sh` injects the reviewer's shapes of rounds 3–4 into a copy (calls from hoh-core outside persistence and from hoh-app, which has no SQLite dependency) and checks that clippy refuses each call site; its log is next to it. The scanner now checks that nothing switches the lint off: all required entries present and no `allow-invalid`, no other clippy configuration, no Cargo `[lints]` or `.cargo/config` rustflags allowing it, no `allow`/`expect` of `clippy::disallowed_methods`, `clippy::style`, `clippy::all` or `warnings` in product sources outside persistence, and no rusqlite feature beyond the known set (new features add write APIs). Bundle test `test_the_clippy_boundary_cannot_be_switched_off`. The name/visibility/alias text rules are removed. |
| M2T01R-17 | high | persistence_scan.py:175 — `use rusqlite::*` did not make `Connection` a SQLite name; a public factory re-exported through hoh-agents let hoh-app write. | Fixed with R-16: glob imports are irrelevant to clippy's resolution; the probe uses `use rusqlite::*` for its leaks. |
| M2T01R-18 | high | persistence_scan.py:154 — every `pub(in super…)` counted as internal, so `pub(in super::super)` in a submodule opened items to the crate root. | Fixed with R-16: visibility no longer matters to the boundary; the probe leaks through `pub(in crate)` in a nested module and the call outside is refused. |
| M2T01R-19 | high | persistence_scan.py:321 — a `macro_rules!` in persistence re-exported with `pub(crate) use` expanded SQLite calls at the crate root while only `#[macro_export]` was refused. | Fixed with R-16: clippy lints the expansion at its call site, where the lint is not allowed (the probe's macro use outside persistence fails the build; clippy reports the span at the definition). |
| M2T01R-20 | medium | mod.rs:297 — `readonly()` was used as the statement class: `BEGIN IMMEDIATE/EXCLUSIVE` (not read-only) were executed and failed as nested, a legitimate `REINDEX` of a table without indexes (read-only) was refused — a regression from `d45de0e`. | Fixed: statements are classified by their leading keyword — `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`END` skipped, `ROLLBACK`/`SAVEPOINT`/`RELEASE`/`VACUUM`/`ATTACH`/`DETACH` refused unexecuted, `SELECT`/`WITH`/`VALUES`/`EXPLAIN`/`PRAGMA`/`REINDEX`/`ANALYZE` run — and the read-only flag is only a fail-closed net for any other keyword. Test `migration_statements_are_classified_by_their_keyword` (BEGIN IMMEDIATE and BEGIN EXCLUSIVE TRANSACTION scripts, REINDEX without indexes and ANALYZE migrate; VACUUM, ATTACH, SAVEPOINT, RELEASE and ROLLBACK are refused with no schema change); it failed on `519defc` (`cannot start a transaction within a transaction`). |
| M2T01R-21 | high | persistence_scan.py:242 — files were found under `crates/*/` only; with hoh-core moved to `engine/hoh-core` the scan covered 6 files and passed. | Fixed: product sources are the `.rs` files of the member directories from `cargo metadata` (tests/, benches/, examples/ excluded), and the clippy boundary itself covers every member regardless of layout. Bundle test `test_members_are_found_from_metadata_and_path_or_include_are_hits` (hoh-core under `engine/`). |

Mutation evidence after the round-4 fixes (30 mutants, `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t01-mutations/`): the first run on `fb95940` showed that stripping a BOM or empty statement before the script's own `BEGIN` was tested only where the read-only net masks it; positive cases (BOM, `;` and comments before `BEGIN`/`BEGIN IMMEDIATE` must migrate) were added. The survivors on the clean fix commit are the two equivalents (bundled defaults) and two fail-closed nets that no SQL reaches: every read-only keyword SQLite has is now classified explicitly, so neither the read-only net for unknown keywords nor the post-execution autocommit check fires. The clippy boundary probe `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t01-clippy-boundary/probe.sh` (log `probe-fb95940.log`): every leak shape of rounds 3–4 refused at its call site in hoh-core and in hoh-app (7 sites, the macro included), and the probe tree without the calls is clean.

## M2-T01 review round 5 (focused, fresh Codex session)

- Agent: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0, session `01a1a554-262b-71a3-bbc6-4a7fc854fa13`, 1096 s; attempt record sha256 `59dd44be4ad465f752ef6f27b6efd0a86f4812485fd9f4b531b89308378d8add`, stdout sha256 `c1a23811bc43cbc3a5793d37d9488c1f2b4c2cf644ec9d90592bc7347a63316a`
- Scope: fix delta `519defc..f0a47f1`, candidate `CAND-61e6d005…`, 648 manifest files matched before and after; the 12 round-4 programs, the member-move case and the provided probe re-run (all refused with the target lint); API and target coverage (CachedStatement, Deref of Transaction/Savepoint, build scripts, cfg(test), proc macros, `Connection::handle` blocked by `unsafe_code = "forbid"`, the `hooks` feature refused by the scan); migration classification (no regression); cargo test (82 + 2 ignored helpers), static.sh and the bundle (349, 1 skip) PASS; mutate.py in a copy (26 of 30 killed, the 4 survivors' reasons confirmed for this build); every finding below reproduced with static.sh rc 0 and a row written by code outside persistence; no model call
- Verdict: **FAIL** (4 high). R-16 to R-21 RESOLVED; R-13, R-14, R-07 and round-1 R-03 PARTIAL through the findings below.

| ID | Severity | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| M2T01R-22 | high | clippy.toml:31 — lazy execution and implicit commit were not listed: `Rows::next()` or iterating `MappedRows` handed to a callback outside persistence wrote a row; so did `set_drop_behavior(Commit)` and dropping the transaction. | Fixed: the list (58 paths) adds `Rows::{next, map, mapped, and_then}`, `FallibleStreamingIterator::{advance, next}` and `FallibleIterator::next` (those traits are used by rusqlite alone in this dependency graph) and `Transaction`/`Savepoint::set_drop_behavior`. Iterating a `MappedRows`/`AndThenRows` goes through `Iterator::next` and cannot be singled out; such an iterator can only be made by persistence (all its constructors are disallowed elsewhere), so the stated boundary is: code outside persistence cannot open SQLite, author or start SQL, step a statement or change how a transaction ends; persistence hands out no SQLite object (docs/22). |
| M2T01R-23 | high | persistence_scan.py:53 — lint names were matched as raw text: Cargo's `disallowed-methods = { level = "allow", priority = 1 }` inherited through `workspace = true`, and `allow(clippy :: disallowed_methods)` kept by `#[rustfmt::skip]` or inside `cfg_attr`, passed. | Fixed: Cargo lint keys are read with `-` as `_`, attributes are compared with whitespace removed (`cfg_attr` included), and the lint's former name `disallowed_method` counts. Bundle test `test_lint_names_are_normalised_and_test_or_example_files_are_not_exempt`. Independently, the canary below fails whenever any lint table, cap or configuration switches the lint off for hoh-core. |

The scan checks these roots from `cargo metadata` and also reads raw identifiers as plain ones. A probe in a copy: a raw-identifier allow, a `cfg_attr` allow and a `macro_rules!`-generated `#[$attr]` allow under `forbid` each fail with E0453. Bundle test `test_every_target_root_forbids_the_lint`. |

No text rule for macro attributes is needed (one was tried and refused the repository's own `schema_enum!`, which forwards doc attributes the same way). |
| M2T01R-29 | high | persistence_scan.py:249 — any path component named `target` was skipped (`src/target/mod.rs`), and a `bin.path` outside the member directory was never read. | Fixed: only cargo's real build output (`target_directory` from `cargo metadata`) is excluded, and the forbid check reads every target root wherever it lies (a root outside the members without the forbid is a hit; covered by `test_every_target_root_forbids_the_lint`). |

Bundle test `test_cargo_configuration_outside_the_repository_is_checked`. docs/22 now states what each part proves: the canary proves the lint in force for hoh-core's library in the environment of the check; the other targets are held by their forbid levels and the configuration checks; replacing cargo, clippy or the compiler themselves is outside a source and configuration check. |

## AC-062-05 threat model: owner decision (2026-11-06)

The owner confirmation asked during M2-T01 (rounds 9, 11 and 18 above: still open) is answered: option A, the static check covers ordinary development errors and accidental violations and deliberate circumvention is left to independent code review; a real defect is not classed as circumvention just because its reproduction uses constructed input. The owner confirmed the principle, not each classification made during M2-T01 (such as run-time-only content and macro-generated cfg in round 11, and M2T01R-57 in round 12): those remain judgments made during development, not the owner's (D2R-06). Recorded in docs/22 (AC-062-05, "范围与限制"); the item is closed.

## CR-006: DB_FULL and fault injection points as no-op hooks (for M2-T12)

CR-006 adds ErrorCode `DB_FULL` (503, retryable; nothing written) and rewrites REQ-036's decision "fault injection points compiled only with a test feature, the production binary contains no injection path", which contradicted AC-062-05's rule that members declare no Cargo features. The review judged the first rewrite a weakening that D1 cannot approve; the project owner then chose, among three options put to them in the main development session (no-op hooks, a dedicated cfg with a double static build, deferring the three fault ACs), the no-op hooks. The product's hook implementations do nothing; every fault-making implementation is test code. Seven scenario fixtures were reworded to match; steps and pass criteria are unchanged. Reviews of the uncommitted CR diff (with the M2-T12 implementation of F4 from round 2 on):

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T12R-01 | high | Claude | With an SSE client connected the host never stopped: graceful shutdown waits for every connection and an event stream only ended when the writer went, which the stream kept alive; SIGTERM left the host holding the lock with host.json and the socket in place; a connection that never sent its header blocked the same way. The tests disconnected subscribers before shutdown. | Fixed: event streams end on the host's stop signal; connections get 5 s and are then ended; tests stop a host with two subscribers (under 2 s), with a half-sent request (under 10 s, the lock free again), and a host process on SIGTERM with a subscriber (exit 0, files removed); each fix's mutant turns its part red. |
| M2T12R-30 | high | Codex | AC-041-04 read the first result only after the retry, so a disconnect before the commit would also pass. | Fixed: before the retry the test checks the command row, its result, one event and the run's move; the retry must carry `Idempotent-Replayed: true` and the stored bytes. |
| M2T12R-31 | high | Codex | AC-062-02 checked events and outbox only: a lost commands row (and its stored result) passed. | Fixed: every 202 is checked against its command row (exact result, run), the run's state and revision, one event and the outbox row pointing at it; every retryable answer must have written nothing. |
| M2T12R-02 | medium | Claude | A constraint failure (e.g. a command_id reused under another key) and the storage fallback put the database's text into the response. | Fixed: fixed messages, the detail to the host's stderr; the command_id clash is 409 CONFLICT; a test checks the body has no database text. |
| M2T12R-03 | medium | Claude | Extractor rejections answered text/plain, not Error bodies (bad `after`, a run id that is not UTF-8, an oversized body); streamEvents answered 400, which openapi does not list. | Fixed: Error bodies within each endpoint's listed codes (submitCommand 400, getRun 404 RUN_NOT_FOUND, streamEvents 409 CONFLICT), unknown routes and methods 404 Error; ADR-016 decision 6; tested. |
| M2T12R-04 | medium | Claude | Untested ADR-016 behaviour: lock held → 69 naming the pid, host.json 0600 and its content written whole, socket 0600, stale socket removed and other files left, shutdown removing files, nothing bound on a failed start, `/healthz` Host check, loopback Origin, Last-Event-ID-only resume. | Fixed: tests for each. |
| M2T12R-05 | low | Claude | Only the first Host/Origin value was checked and an absolute-form target's authority ignored. | Fixed: exactly one Host, at most one Origin, a target's authority must be the bound address (none on the socket); tested. |
| M2T12R-06 | low | Claude | AC-062-02's "increasing and unique" was a tautology (seq is the rowid). | Fixed: the commands one client sent in turn must have increasing seqs. |
| M2T12R-07 | low | Claude | AC-062-06's /p4 was under the temporary directory, tmpfs on this VM, while the Given says ext4. | Fixed: /p4 under the target directory and its file system asserted ext4. |
| M2T12R-08 | low | Claude | A busy port left the socket behind; a failed host.json released the lock while serving; socket bound under the umask before chmod. | Fixed: the port first, a failed host.json stops serving first; the umask window and `.hoh` mode stay a follow-up (the token still guards it). |
| M2T12R-09 | low | Claude | ADR-016's error table disagreed with the code on current_revision; STALE_REVISION printed `Some(1)`. | Fixed: the table follows the code, the message says the revision plainly. |

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T12R-120 | high | Codex | A writer that panics dropped what it kept (the lock) on unwind while the rest of its host still ran: a next host started at epoch 2 while the old one still answered with its token, and the old `shutdown` then removed the new host's host.json and socket by path. | Fixed: what the writer keeps is leaked, not dropped, when its thread unwinds, so the lock stays held until the process ends (the old host's writes fail with 503, no other host takes over); a stopping host removes only its own host.json (by token) and the socket it bound (by inode). Tests: a panicking commit hook keeps the project locked before and after `shutdown`; another host's files are left; mutants (kept dropped on unwind, files removed by path) turn them red. |
| M2T12R-121 | medium | Codex | The replay test wrote 60,000 events outside the writer, so the watermark stayed 0 and no replay happened: it tested an idle stream, and the developer's explanation of the surviving mutant was wrong. | Fixed: the events are followed by a command through the writer (watermark past them) and the test waits for replayed data before the stop. With a real replay: a client that keeps reading ends with the stop in under 3 s (without the per-step check 5.0 s, the mutant is now killed); a client that reads nothing is not polled, so its connection is ended after the grace (new test, ADR-016 decision 12 wording). |

Test runs a host process whose writer panics: GONE, exit 1, then the next host at epoch 2; mutants (run ignoring the writer, health always 200) red. |

Test: removing host.lock does not free the project (red when the file is locked instead). |
| M2T12R-153 | low, outside the diff | Claude | A failed `local_addr`/token after the socket was bound, a socket whose inode could not be read, and a dropped Host left host.json or the socket. | Fixed: the port and token come before the socket; a socket that cannot be identified fails the start; host.json and the socket are a guard removed once at the stop, a failed start or the drop (only this host's). Test: a dropped Host leaves no files (red without the guard's drop). |
| M2T12R-154 | low, outside the diff | Claude | Tests that start a child process did not reap it on a failed assertion or early return. | Fixed: a guard sends SIGTERM, waits, kills and reaps on every way out. |

The CI failure of `a_failed_start_leaves_nothing_served`: tests that start another host in a project this process just let go retry a refusal (69) for up to 5 s, since a child process of a parallel test can share the lock until it execs; tests that expect the refusal start the host themselves.

## M2-T04 Outbox delivery, receipt dedupe, HCP messages and handoffs (PR #30, branch `task/M2-T04-outbox-delivery`)

### Round 1 (two fresh sessions of two agents, complementary focus)

Candidate m2t04-001 (`b98c214`, tree `df0cc7c`, `CAND-4c7e52fc…`); checks unit, integration, static rc=0 with the source unchanged (TEST-022-01/03/04/05 and TEST-062-03 PASS). Scope: the diff `9149a62..b98c214`; Codex on delivery semantics and transactions (ids -01..49), Claude on conformance and the handoff (ids -50..99).

- Codex: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0 (default model), session `01a1b38e-b5c3-7800-b1c2-eb8a01d05164`, 561 s; attempt record sha256 `0edfc819b3cd06bf3c087cf39ec78d5acc3e16cd325b57dc3fcefc9f8b30fcf9`, stdout sha256 `53c19c57368483bb618f33d9d1e2bc1898f1e4eaf18d10a7b05cb04ba15a93ca`. Verdict **FAIL** (5 high, 1 medium), each with a probe experiment.
- Claude: `claude` 2.1.319, model claude-opus-5-5, session `9d5732fc-d749-4d68-b9c2-7231fb6cf0fc`, 738 s; attempt record sha256 `eec3fd0df7b8f6201e2fa521201da6a14cc2556a50007ecb017b51493f2921ad`, stdout sha256 `7033e22a254683cd371f7e22bd6b3079732cd11e19025bef1899fdfc1f5ddaf8`. Verdict **FAIL** (1 high, 8 medium, 5 low).

Codex's -01..03 are one class: the claim/settle protocol had no delivery generation. Design correction (ADR-018 decision 4): one row is claimed just before its delivery; a row whose deliveries are spent is dead-lettered when it is next due; a settlement names its generation, an answer from any generation makes an unsettled row ACKED, a failure counts only for the current generation; only rows holding an HCP message are claimed.

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Test: the late failure before and after the success leaves the message ACKED, no dead-letter event; mutant ignoring the generation red. |
| M2T04R-02 | high | Codex | A batch claim counted a delivery for every row of the batch, so a cancelled tick spent deliveries of rows never handed to the transport. | Fixed: one claim per delivery. Test: ticks cancelled while `a` hangs; the transport sees a, a, a, b; b is ACKED with one delivery. |
| M2T04R-03 | high | Codex | An expired DELIVERING row was taken again past the limit, so deliveries cut off by a crash or a cancel never dead-lettered. | Fixed: a due row at the limit is dead-lettered in the claim, with its event, once. Same test: `a` DEAD_LETTER at 3, one event; mutant keeping spent rows red. |
| M2T04R-04 | high | Codex | A verification handoff with a null envelope candidate skipped the candidate fence; package fields (git_tree, spec_revision, task, run) were not fenced. | Fixed: `Message::check_payload` (the package agrees with its envelope: run, task, role, epoch, spec, and a verification's candidate with a non-null envelope candidate) for queueing, transport and consume; the fence compares the package's spec revision and the candidate's whole identity with the current values. Tests per case; mutants red. |
| M2T04R-05 | high | Codex | The dispatcher dead-lettered the runtime notices that decide, the scheduling round and PauseRun put in the outbox. | Fixed: only rows holding an HCP message are claimed; notices stay PENDING for their own consumer (the attempt starter and host loop, M2-T05/T06; ADR-018 decision 4). Test; mutant red. |

The answer carries a guard (`Claimed`) that returns the claim to the writer when it is dropped before `Claimed::start`, wherever it is (in the writer's reply, unread in the channel, in the dispatcher); it holds only a weak handle to the writer's queue.

Verdict **PASS** (5 low). It confirmed the new tests are deterministic (tokio 1.53.3 drops a delivered value in Receiver::drop on the dropping thread).

The fourth finding of the class, in the claim's return path. Analysis (ADR-018 decision 4): a delivery must count when its row is handed to the transport (else a hung transport is never counted, -03), and a hand-over that writes to the store can always lose its reply to a cancel (-100, -200); the guard's hand-over (`Claimed::start`) is in memory, so the only weakness left was the return path, which relied on the writer's queue and on the writer running. Design correction: the writer keeps a registry of outstanding claims; a dropped guard moves its entry to a returned list (no channel); the writer undoes returned claims before every job, keeping the ones that fail for the next; at close it undoes every claim not handed over, before it lets the database go; `start` refuses a claim taken back.

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T04R-300 | high | Codex | The undo of a returned claim met SQLITE_BUSY and was lost (three times: DEAD_LETTER, never delivered). | Fixed by the design correction: a failed undo stays returned and is retried before the next job. Test with another connection holding the database past an 80 ms busy timeout; mutants (no undo before jobs, a failed undo dropped) red. |
| M2T04R-301 | medium | Codex | A guard alive when the store closed could not return its claim. | Fixed: the writer undoes every outstanding claim at close, before the connection and the lock go; `start` then gives None. Test; mutants (no sweep, start ignoring the registry) red. |
| M2T04R-350 | low | Claude | With the channel return, the -100 test's barrier could run before the undo. | The registry undoes before each job; the test now waits two jobs and says why. |
| M2T04R-351 | low | Claude | The never-read test did not assert its precondition (the claim ran). | Fixed: it asserts DELIVERING at 1 before dropping the tick. |
| M2T04R-352 | low | Claude | The exact-float replay test tells a reparse comparison apart only with `-p hoh-core` (a workspace build turns on float_roundtrip). | The test says so; the planned integration check runs the workspace, so the gate cannot catch that regression; accepted (the product build has float_roundtrip too). |
| M2T04R-353 | low | Claude | Round 3 left two parts of -254 without a disposition: a failed undo was swallowed, and the "nobody waits" branch had no test. | Both gone with the design correction: a failed undo is retried; the branch was removed (the registry returns such a claim anyway). ADR-018 listed one residual; round 5 (-452) found that list incomplete, corrected there. |
| M2T04R-354 | low | Claude | A stale comment of round 2 stood above the new one. | Removed with the rewrite. |

Candidate m2t04-005 (`84a82a8`, tree `c842ac0`, `CAND-199d872b…`); checks unit, integration, static rc=0 with the source unchanged; CI green. Scope: the fix diff `f551e81..84a82a8`; ids Codex -400..449, Claude -450..499.

- Codex: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0 (default model), session `01a1b3f2-32e4-7e73-8510-3349836b6a33`, 502 s; attempt record sha256 `f684f7d03cce53fcc174b1447fcc7ab41e38dad3e0a3400975e80045ff450253`, stdout sha256 `614861dbf5a364f06134abc5295572a5c1b500844343abfbef48d10eafadd121`. Verdict **FAIL** (1 high, 2 medium).
- Claude: `claude` 2.1.319, model claude-opus-5-5, session `b0cafc22-6581-41af-a07e-cd858fd2c038`, 878 s; attempt record sha256 `0f7fbbac8e59fa28db05bf036faa673211140b0ec5f5a4945ad06f1a4a2c6c7e`, stdout sha256 `8aae6e13f91fb06aac7eb61c05b8829d43c0afbfc155f058a74c0c97d8626a84`. Verdict **PASS** (2 medium, 4 low).

The fifth round of the class; both reviewers found the same remaining gap: the undo of a returned claim was a write of its own beside the claim, so a re-claim could overtake a failed or pending undo, and undoing before every job lengthened every write's busy wait. Design correction (ADR-018 decision 4): the next claim undoes the returned claims in its own IMMEDIATE transaction before it selects a row (a claim that cannot run fails whole and keeps them), and skips messages with a live guard; no other job undoes anything; the writer thread closes the registry however it ends.

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T04R-400 | high | Codex | A row with an unread or unreturned guard, or whose undo had failed, could be claimed again and counted (three unread claims across their backoff: DEAD_LETTER with no delivery; two live guards undone only once). | Fixed by the design correction: undo in the claim's transaction; live guards' messages skipped. Tests: three unread ticks across the backoff leave one pending claim, then one delivery (ACKED at 1); mutants (no undo in the claim, live rows claimed) red. |
| M2T04R-450 | medium | Claude | Same gap: a failed undo overtaken by a re-claim counted a delivery for good (ACKED at 2 after one delivery). | Fixed with -400. Test: a claim fails busy while another connection holds the database, the clock passes the backoff, the next claim undoes then claims: delivered once, ACKED at 1; mutant (a failed claim drops its returns) red. |
| M2T04R-401 | medium | Codex | Undoing before every job added a busy timeout per return to every write. | Fixed: only a claim undoes. Test: with a return waiting and the database held for 1.5 busy timeouts, a commit gets DB_BUSY. |
| M2T04R-451 | medium | Claude | Same as -401 (REQ-062's DB_BUSY limit). | Fixed with -401. |
| M2T04R-402 | medium | Codex | After a writer panic a live guard could still start. | Fixed: the registry is closed when the writer thread ends, by a panic too; test with a panicking commit hook; mutants red. |

### Round 6 (two fresh sessions of two agents, the fix diff)

Candidate m2t04-006 (`ca3dc36`, tree `3f48e43`, `CAND-85fe2006…`); checks unit (9 PASS), integration (34 PASS), static rc=0 with the source unchanged. Scope: the fix diff `84a82a8..ca3dc36`; ids Codex -500..549, Claude -550..599. Both PASS.

- Claude: `claude` 2.1.319, model claude-opus-5-5, session `0f77e1d7-b06c-4785-b345-c92cd72773bf`, 865 s; attempt record sha256 `0e27a13b9bdca0f6928e59c0fcf5f5d83567824bce2d9c96521e16dbcb4372e6`, stdout sha256 `bf37dfe16b74b21dde606298fb02b52719e5ba0ac194794a2360707fa381d709`.

The sixth finding of the class, a medium. Codex judged the protocol and the listed residuals sound; the remaining gap is the undo against a late answer of an earlier delivery. The design correction (ADR-018 decision 4) enumerates the states first. While generation k is returned and not yet undone, nothing settles k (it never started) and nothing claims the row (the next claim undoes first). Only earlier generations' settlements touch the row: a failure is ignored, and a delivery makes it ACKED. So the undo finds the row at k, DELIVERING or ACKED, and nothing else. It takes k off the count in both; only DELIVERING goes back to PENDING.

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T04R-500 | medium | Codex | A returned claim whose row was made ACKED first by a late answer of an earlier delivery was never uncounted (ACKED at 2 after one delivery), by the next claim or at close. | Fixed: the undo matches DELIVERING or ACKED at the returned generation, takes the count back in both, and restores PENDING only from DELIVERING. Test: two messages delivered, their second claims returned unstarted, the first answers settle them ACKED; the next claim, and the close, leave each ACKED at 1 with one receipt; mutants red. |
| M2T04R-550 | low | Claude | A test comment still said returns are undone before any job. | Fixed: the next claim undoes it in its own transaction. |
| M2T04R-551 | low | Claude | A third copy of the store-opening code and a dead binding in the panic test. | Fixed: one `open_with(dir, Options)` helper; the binding is gone. |
| M2T04R-552 | low | Claude | The -401/-451 timing test had no replayable red mutant. | Added: `undo-before-every-job` in the round-6 mutants, red. |
| M2T04R-553 | low | Claude | The no-stacking test did not assert that the second and third claims ran. | Fixed: after the barrier it polls them and asserts each finished with an empty Tick. The live-skip mutant first recorded here dropped the bound `?3` and went red on a parameter-count error; corrected in round 7 (-600, -650), it now fails at the new precondition assertion. |

## M2-T05 Startup reconcile, effect ledger, lease fencing and the kill -9 recovery matrix (PR #31, branch `task/M2-T05-recovery`)

### Round 1 (two fresh sessions of two agents, complementary focus)

Candidate m2t05-001 (`96b3a2a`, tree `22f5da3`, `CAND-1564b72a…`). Checks with the source unchanged: unit (9 PASS), integration (42 PASS), recovery (5 PASS: TEST-036-02, TEST-041-01/02/03/05) rc=0; static rc=1 (persistence_scan: the new test root lacked its forbid attributes, -50). CI product failed once: TEST-036-02's restarted host was ended by SIGTERM's default action (see the signal race below). Scope: the diff `7428a60..96b3a2a`; Codex on process lifetime, recovery semantics and transactions (-01..49), Claude on the tests, the recovery check and the documents (-50..99). Both FAIL.

- Codex: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0 (default model), session `01a1b489-77bf-77c0-9d4b-67ea558b498e`, 655 s; attempt record sha256 `eaf2fe19c4109aea013c4b8e0c8e7e307ff918dbef59a9f5563b9e417ad3bfa7`, stdout sha256 `3177019463300db1caaee2e84f3d4c221a2f4b0151c19b4b5f5ad24e129df2e4`.
- Claude: `claude` 2.1.319, model claude-opus-5-5, session `0bb52c20-97d6-4388-87b8-1d0e9475051b`, 1001 s; attempt record sha256 `6bda9c76227cb1015e56ad44f43a98157f67cb69206cfefc334d4c91752ec31e`, stdout sha256 `a5110f9d315074ababd15c42ca7b1bd8d8fbd28861a815b6c77608ab83237e51`.

The criticals are one class: a writer slot was let go on an absence of records, not on a proof that the holder's process cannot run. Design correction (ADR-019 decision 8): agents start at a start gate, opened only after one transaction records the process's identity and the STARTED work effect, so an unrecorded identity proves the agent never ran; a leaderless group, or a RUNNING attempt with no process record, is uncertain (no signal, the slot kept, a diagnostic, looked at again later); a settle aborts and waits for the attempt's own task before it reads the row again; a lost completion is settled like an expired lease; the work's outcome is recorded and disposed of by.

Test: kill at the new `AfterSpawn` point, 5 rounds: the attempt ends CANCELLED and its key is never counted; mutant red. |

Tests: unit (a leaderless group is found) and a host one (no signal, A1 kept RUNNING with its lease, T2 not granted). |

Test: an executor whose start takes 800 ms; the attempt's work never runs before T2 is granted. |

Mutants of the fixes, replayable: `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t05-r1-fix-mutations/` (8; 7 red at their target assertions). `abort-without-await` survives: a start overtaken by a settle cannot run even without the await, because its gate is dropped unopened and its running transaction's STARTING -> RUNNING compare-and-set fails once the settle ended the attempt; the await is kept as defense in depth (nothing of this host writes for the attempt while it is settled), and the test asserts the property (the work never runs). A test for a RUNNING attempt with no process record was added with the batch.

Found by the CI run and fixed in the same batch: `hoh_app::host::run` registered SIGTERM after `Host::start` wrote host.json; a stop in that window ended the host by the default action. The signals are taken before the start; `HostHooks::announced` (a no-op point) lets a test send SIGTERM there (exit 0, files removed).

The single-writer class a second time: the round-1 principle was not applied on every path that ends an attempt. Design correction v2 (ADR-019 decision 8): only two paths let a slot go (the runner's end of an attempt, and the reconcile's or a settle's disposition), and both need the same proof: a whole identity before the gate (a pid recorded without boot or start time is never a mismatch, only uncertain); the leader's pidfd held from its start until its group is confirmed empty, the normal end emptying the group through it; one inspection shared by the reconcile and the settle; an attempt's task removing its entry however it ends and settling at once on an error; repeated findings not written again.

Test: a live process recorded with no boot or start time keeps its slot, no signal; mutant red. |
| M2T05R-101 | critical | Codex | A leader that ended normally let its slot go with a member of its group still running. | Fixed: after the leader ends, the runner empties its group through the pinned pidfd before the attempt ends; if it cannot, `runner.unreaped` and the attempt stays RUNNING. Test: a leader that leaves a SIGTERM-ignoring member; the group is empty inside the granting round; mutant red. |
| M2T05R-102 | high | Codex | An attempt task that failed left its entry, whose lease was renewed forever. | Fixed: the task removes its entry however it ends and, on an error, settles at once (`runner_error`) with its pin; renewal covers only running tasks. Test: a runner write fails at the receipt and at the OBSERVED effect; both attempts end and T2 follows; mutant red. |

Also found and fixed while testing the batch: the tests' FakeClock was frozen at the test's start while an attempt's end is recorded at the system time, so a writer slot released a second later looked held to a round judged at the frozen time (flaky T2 grants). The FakeClock now moves forward from the system time (a jump added by the test); in the product both are the system clock. Runner writes now pass the commit fault point too (`before_commit`, a no-op in the product), so a test can fail them.

Mutants of the fixes, replayable: `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t05-r2-fix-mutations/` (5, all red). Workspace 523 passed, 0 failed; static and bundle rc 0; the recovery suite 3/3 stable.

The single-writer class a third time, now in a premise of v2: a pidfd keeps the process, not its number (verified here: a zombie answers `pidfd_send_signal(0)`, a reaped one ESRCH, its fdinfo `Pid: -1`). Design correction v3 (ADR-019 decision 8): a group is signalled only while its leader still holds its pid (alive or a zombie); the executor empties the leader's group before it reaps the leader and reports it (`Outcome::group_gone`, as the hoh-agents substrate does), and the runner ends an attempt only on that report; a scan that fails is never an empty group; the pin is gone.

### Round 4 (two fresh sessions of two agents, the fix diff)

Candidate m2t05-004 (`1c4c5ef`, tree `5734f6f`, `CAND-d5d8daed…`); checks unit 9, integration 39, static (58/58) and recovery 5 PASS, rc 0. Scope: the fix diff `0859e3b..1c4c5ef`; ids Codex -300..349, Claude -350..399. Codex FAIL; Claude PASS.

- Codex: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0 (default model), session `01a1b51c-580e-7902-885b-39e2747bb0da`, 672 s; attempt record sha256 `12fb281d016ee750b3be72a5ce814b860505731dee93ab717ce49a837c2aeb93`, stdout sha256 `2d2bb0d177f104f3af8ed82a922fa124806bdc07ff4869fcc341b8b851c9664d`.
- Claude: `claude` 2.1.319, model claude-opus-5-5, session `c398da10-0132-4f9f-97bd-6c00466f4302`, 1494 s; attempt record sha256 `699442cd8691f0bbece5d4cf9cf80776719ba61433130b2a51eddc045dbed21d`, stdout sha256 `6f3f78eaacacb6d907de5fd8f26beb1309e88f98eebf2bb584e9b6210ab8872f`.

The single-writer class a fourth time, in both halves of v3: emptiness read from a /proc scan (no atomic snapshot), and a group number taken to be unprotected once the leader is reaped (its members keep it in use). Design correction v4 (ADR-019 decision 8), written and verified before the code (here: a zombie leader alone keeps `kill(-N,0)` at 0, its reap makes it ESRCH, a reaped leader's member keeps it at 0): the group is the attempt's from the leader's verification while the number stays in use; emptiness is the kernel's `kill(-N, 0)` = ESRCH; every group signal goes right after a look that shows the group held (leader unreaped, or members left and the previous look at most `HELD_FOR` = 100 ms earlier); a group not watched that closely since its leader's reap is lost track of (no signal, not reaped, slot kept). Accepted residual, bounded and documented: a signal by number reaches another group only if the group empties and its number is handed out again (the cyclic allocator comes round to it) within one look interval or between the last look and the signal. The exact fix, `pidfd_send_signal` with `PIDFD_SIGNAL_PROCESS_GROUP` (Linux 6.9+), is out of reach without unsafe code (AC-062-05): rustix 1.1.6, the newest, passes no flags, and nix 0.31.3 has no such call; follow-up when a safe binding exists.

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T05R-300 | critical | Codex | A /proc scan is no atomic snapshot: a listed member forks a new one and exits before its stat is read, the scan reads the group empty, the stop says reaped and the slot goes with the new member alive. | Fixed by design v4: emptiness is `identity::group_empty` (`kill(-N, 0)` = ESRCH, the kernel's own look-up, zombies included); no /proc read on any emptiness path (the scan stays for diagnostics and evidence). Test: a churning group (each member starts the next, then exits) is never seen empty over ~450 looks in 1 s, then stopped and seen empty; meanwhile a whole scan reads it empty ~15 % of the time (shown in the test output, 69-84 of ~460 in three runs). Mutant (the scan back in `look`) red. |
| M2T05R-301 | high | Codex | `holds_group` and the `killpg` are two steps: the leader can be reaped and the number reused in between. | Fixed by design v4 within what safe Rust can do: each group signal follows a look at once, and the look's proof is the leader unreaped or, after its reap, a group watched every 10 ms with no gap over 100 ms; beyond that it is lost track of (no signal). The remaining window (the group emptying and its number handed out again by the cyclic allocator inside one look interval, or between the look and the signal) is the accepted, documented residual; the struct-pid signal that closes it needs unsafe code or a binding rustix does not have (follow-up). Test: a group not looked at for more than `HELD_FOR` after its leader's reap gets no signal, its stop is `[lost_track]`, not reaped, the member alive. Mutant (no bound) red. |
| M2T05R-351 | medium | Claude | v3 refused to SIGKILL a group whose leader was reaped though its members keep the number in use: a self-made regression (the common case: a leader that exits on SIGTERM, a member that ignores it). | Fixed by design v4: a watched group is signalled by number after the leader's reap. The unit test now expects the member killed (`[sigterm, sigkill, reaped]`, group empty). Mutant (v3's refusal) red. |
| M2T05R-302 | medium | Codex | The leaderless finding carried the member count, so a fixture whose members churn wrote a new settle on retries; the one-settle assertion was flaky. | Fixed with -350. |
| M2T05R-350 | medium | Claude | Same as -302 (8 of 30 runs failed; 0 of 30 with a stable basis). | Fixed: the basis is `leader_gone_group_not_empty`, from the kernel look-up (no member list); the test is unchanged and passed 20 of 20 runs here. |
| M2T05R-303 | low | Codex | The executor contract said "empty the group, then reap"; the substrate SIGKILLs the group while the leader is a zombie, reaps, then waits for ESRCH. | Fixed: `Executor::start` and `Outcome::group_gone` say the substrate's order; the test executor `reaping` follows it (group SIGKILL, reap, `group_empty`). |
| M2T05R-359 | low | Claude | ADR steps 3 and 6 still spoke of the pin, v2 item 4 was not marked withdrawn, the injection list lacked Settling, and the contract wording was off (-303). | Fixed in ADR-019 decision 8 (steps 3 and 6, v2 item 4, v3 items 1-3 and 6 marked, Settling listed). |

Mutant (no grace watch) red. |

Mutant (a failed inspection taken as gone) red. |

The single-writer class a fifth time, now in two observations of v4's implementation (its model stands): a read that failed was taken as a negative observation, and an observation's moment was taken after it. Design correction v5 (ADR-019 decision 8): an observation proves only what it read, at a moment taken before the read.

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T05R-400 | critical | Codex | `unreaped()` folded an fdinfo read error (EMFILE), a parse failure or another value into "reaped"; with the leader moved out of its group, `look` read the empty old group as Empty and the stop reported reaped with the leader alive. | Fixed by v5: the leader's state is three-valued (`Leader::Unreaped` only on its own pid, `Reaped` only on -1, else `Unknown`); Unknown is Lost (no signal, not reaped, slot kept); Empty needs the leader known reaped and `kill(-N, 0)` = ESRCH. Tests: the fdinfo field's meanings; a look whose fd shows no `Pid:` is Lost and its stop `[lost_track]`, not reaped, though its group is empty. Mutants (an unknown read as reaped; Unknown as Empty) red. |
| M2T05R-401 | high | Codex | `find` took `held` after the start-time read, so a pause there counted as a fresh look; -301 not closed. | Fixed by v5: the moment is taken before the start-time read (and in `look` before its reads). Test through a private seam (`find_with`): the leader dies and is reaped right after the read and HELD_FOR passes before the look: Lost, the member untouched. Mutant (the moment taken after the read) red. |
| M2T05R-450 | medium | Claude | `presence`'s Leaderless/Gone judgement had no churn test; putting the scan back survived. | Test: a relaying group whose leader is reaped, inspected every 1 ms for 1 s, is Leaderless every time. Mutant (the scan in `presence`) red. |
| M2T05R-451 | low | Claude | Held's first leg (an unreaped leader) was never asserted, nor `HELD_FOR`'s value. | Test: a zombie leader not looked at for HELD_FOR + 50 ms is still Held, and once reaped with nothing left the group is Empty (this pins the fdinfo reading on CI's kernel); `HELD_FOR == 100 ms` asserted. Mutants (the leg never seen; HELD_FOR widened) red. |
| M2T05R-452 | low | Claude | Between the settle's inspection and its first look came two commits and the executor's cancel; past HELD_FOR with the leader reaped, the group was lost track of (liveness only). | Fixed by v5: inspection, native cancel, grace and stop run in one blocking section with no write in between; the step events are then committed together, in their order (AC-041-02's order unchanged); `Executor::cancel` only asks and returns. The first cut wrote them one by one after the stop and broke `a_settle_cut_short_is_done_again_by_another_pass` in the self-test (6 of 6: a pass cut short had already stopped the group, so the next found it gone and recorded no reap): a pass's events are now one commit, so a pass whose commit fails leaves no record, and that test asserts this for the first commit failing (one pass recorded, its inspection `gone: no_process`; that it pins the single commit was too strong a claim: round 6's -552 pins it). Test: every commit takes 200 ms once the settle begins, the cancel kills only the leader and its executor reaps it at once leaving a member that ignores SIGTERM: the member still gets SIGTERM and SIGKILL, the group is seen empty, A1 lets its slot go. Mutant (a 200 ms gap after the cancel) red. |
| M2T05R-453 | low | Claude | The residual's argument was imprecise: the look-to-kill window is bounded only by scheduling; the cyclic allocator may be just before N after a wrap; a reused number must also form its own group N; PIDFD_SIGNAL_PROCESS_GROUP needs 6.9 while Ubuntu 24.04's GA kernel is 6.8. | Rewritten in ADR-019 (v4 item 4): all four points; the follow-up probes the kernel at run time and keeps the watching as the fallback. |

Mutants of the fixes, replayable: `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t05-r5-fix-mutations/` (7, all red at their target assertions, `apply-r5.txt`, replayed again in `apply-r5-final.txt`, which predates the kill-matrix assertion change and so is not on the committed tree: round 6's -554; round 6 replays every set on its committed tree); round 4's 9 replayed on the result (`apply-r4-final.txt`: 8 red; -300's pattern moved with -400's rewrite of `look`, so it is rebuilt in this round's set as `r4-300-scan-emptiness-rebuilt`, red). 8 of this round's set in all.

Found in the self-test of the fixes, outside the findings: `a_failure_committed_before_a_crash_stays_a_failure` failed once in 14 suite runs (not again in 12 alone, 8 suites, 30 parallel copies). By the code: another attempt can be STARTING when the host is killed at AfterEffectCommitted, and the reconcile rightly ends it CANCELLED (its work never started), while the test took every ended attempt to be FAILED. The assertion then required each ended attempt to be FAILED or CANCELLED and at least one FAILED, and printed the state read (too weak, as round 6's -550 showed: it now judges each attempt by its recorded move).

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T19R-350 | medium | Codex | v2's latest record missed host-clock times with no event (an outbox claim, a receipt), so a later life could still be behind them. | v2 withdrawn (below); with no clock floor there is no list to complete. |
| M2T19R-351 | medium | Codex | v2 took max() over RFC 3339 text before parsing; a valid non-canonical time (an offset) made the record an hour early. | v2 withdrawn. |
| M2T19R-352 | medium | Codex | v2's lead in u64 nanoseconds saturated for a record ~584 years ahead while reporting the full move. | v2 withdrawn. |
| M2T19R-400 | medium | Claude | Once moved, v2's clock lead is inherited by every later life (its own stamps are the next latest record) and shrinks only by downtime: stored UTC fields stay ahead of real time, understated by the ADR. | v2 withdrawn. |
| M2T19R-401 | low | Claude | The store has two clocks (the writer's commit times by the system clock, the rest by the host clock), so v2's latest record mixed them and its coverage depended on listing every host-clock stamp. | v2 withdrawn; v3 compares no time across lives except the one same-life interval below. |
| M2T19R-402 | low | Claude | v2's attempts.ended_at source was pinned by no test. | v2 withdrawn. |

The fourth round of one class (-100, -200, -300/-301, -350..402), so the approach changed again rather than patching v2: design correction v3 (ADR-020 decision 4). Stored times are UTC and are judged afresh after a restart (docs/14); nothing is moved and the host's time never leaves its wall-clock anchor. The one protection is for the budget: an attempt is charged no less than its own life recorded it ran, from started_at to its work's OBSERVED ended_at (one life's clock; a lease time is no evidence of running), and a reconcile whose time is behind the attempt's start records `clock_behind_ms` on its `reconcile.attempt` event. v2's code (`Clock::not_before`, the lead, `Reader::latest_recorded`, the start-event field) and its three tests are withdrawn, and so is its mutant set (`.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t19-r4-fix-mutations/`, kept as the record of round 4). The accepted residual is the class itself, bounded and listed in decision 4: a wall clock corrected by δ between two lives shifts the earlier life's targets (next_retry_at, lease expiry, the outbox's next_retry_at) by δ once each, and a cross-life duration with no recorded end is under-charged after a backward correction by at most what the attempt ran in the earlier life; the mitigation is the time synchronisation before the host starts (M2T19R-253, M2-T14). Store test (A1 RUNNING since 01:00:00 by the earlier life, reconciled at 00:02:00: with an OBSERVED end at 01:00:05 it ends at 00:02:00, its writer lease is released then and T2 runs at once (-300), its retry is 00:02:00 + 120 s, 5 s are charged (-200) and 3 480 000 ms are recorded behind; with no record nothing is charged and the reversal is recorded; a reconcile not behind charges its own time and records nothing).

Mutants, replayed on the round-5 fixes (`07dc470`, tree `c3191ba`), each set in one run with its `apply.txt` headed by that commit and tree: `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t19-mutations/` 16/17 red (the documented survivor; `m19-no-wall-accrual` retargeted to the new call), `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t19-r1-fix-mutations/` 13/13, `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t19-r2-fix-mutations/` 4/4, `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t19-r3-fix-mutations/` 2/2, `.hoh-dev/evidence/m2t19-r5-fix-mutations/` 4/4 red (no same-life lower bound; the reconcile passing no record; no behind record; the reversal measured the other way).

- Codex: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0 (default model), session `01a1b8f1-afeb-79c0-b21b-35daa823bbc8`, 532 s; attempt record sha256 `eea3b42734369f15511168a42f2b16612ac4db3d9b906b4d76b17cdccee74dc5`, stdout sha256 `07c15524342e7d5e7ba81812230e145e4f6bf0c1e0198338942ad6a2f2ca302a`.
- Claude: `claude` 2.1.319, model claude-opus-5-5, session `f21a2b4d-2267-4250-8502-27d620662812`, 993 s; attempt record sha256 `300a9118e03b62d1bb4f58a4a7d1022a5a6680ed3971fd11d19a2c9602eff7b9`, stdout sha256 `91f17ab2d20d31fe35b925035aaa16cb144b474d700900fdc067c46b07eb7d29`.

-200 is the third finding of the class of -01 and -100. Codex kept v3's structure (one proof, four paths) and named a per-attempt cgroup as the fix. A cgroup needs the host in a delegated cgroup, which a host started by a client from a login session is not in (its session scope is root's); that is M2-T14's systemd unit with `Delegate=`. Unprivileged user and PID namespaces are refused on this VM (`kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1`). So the design correction v4, written before the code, makes the /proc scan itself sound against the race: it sweeps every pid allocated while it looked (`ns_last_pid`), so a marked process alive at its end was looked at or was forked by a marked process looked at; more scans are not the remedy. The cgroup stays the follow-up for the stated residual (a marker cleared or unreadable).

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T06R-200 | critical | Codex | A /proc listing is no snapshot: a marked process that forks and exits between the listing and its environment read leaves its child unseen; two such hand-offs gave Done while the last one wrote. | ADR-021 8d v4: `ns_last_pid` before the listing; the listed processes' markers read; then every pid allocated since examined (threads skipped), batch by batch, until no allocation happened meanwhile (64 batches or a span over 65 536 pids at most, else incomplete). Unit test: between the listing and the reads a real marked process forks a child and exits (a test-only hook); the child is found and pinned; red without the sweep. |
| M2T06R-201 | medium | Codex | A pass whose looks stayed Held but whose signals reached nothing (group empty, scan incomplete, nothing pinned) tried again at once, without waiting. | A step counts for the ladder's times whether or not it reached a process (only what reached one is recorded as sent): the pass waits its graces and ends Unreaped at the second SIGKILL (8d). - Codex: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0 (default model), session `01a1b9f8-6e00-7850-8d15-f99a72675ce7`, 774 s; attempt record sha256 `58009160ce1f8669b6685211ba85e36558ef123314db13542bd4db5f08eb2165`, stdout sha256 `dfa1650dbd4f25a5142dcb1040d2db97c7fce6a9f66f63a335409282536346ae`.

Both found the same critical: a same-user process can leave a delegated cgroup (write its pid into another cgroup's `cgroup.procs`, or have the user's systemd make it another scope, as tmux 3.6 does for its panes), so `populated 0` alone does not prove an attempt's processes ended. No unprivileged mechanism can confine a same-user process (another uid, or a user namespace, which this platform refuses). Design correction 8d v6 keeps the cgroup (atomic, through fork, setsid, threads and environment changes) and adds the marker scan to the proof; the remaining residual is a process that both leaves the cgroup and hides its marker, stated in ADR-021 and, by CR-012 (CR-003 D1, reviewed below), in REQ-043 decision 3.

-700 is the seventh instance of the class (an attempt's process outliving it), in the -600 fix: "no process has its group number" was read as "the group cannot come back", but process n alive in another group can make group n again. -900 is the ninth instance of the class, and -950 a tenth form of it: both need the host to see a view of the system other than the attempt's (a mount over the recorded cgroup path, a bind over `/proc/<pid>`), which an unprivileged user gets only by putting the host into new namespaces. The correction closes the class rather than the instance: each attempt's tracking records the namespaces its recording host saw (pid, mount, cgroup, user), and a host that sees others proves nothing of it; in one set of namespaces covering `/proc` or the cgroup mount needs privilege over that mount namespace, outside the model (stated in ADR-021 8d v7). The cgroup is also judged only through the directories recorded at its making, so an absence counts only inside a checked parent and a directory made later at its path proves nothing.

Tests: a directory in the cgroup's place saying `populated 0`, an absence below a replaced parent, no witness (each red: Done); a removal proved; another boot; the witness's round trip; a foreign record, and the reconcile and the cancel on one (red without the check); the delegated-scope tests pass through the checked directories. |

The round-9 audit list named only the kernel's answers; it omitted the identity's `OtherStart` and `OtherBoot`, which rest on /proc's content. |

- Codex: `codex` codex-cli 0.166.0 (default model), session `01a1bd48-3267-7442-99a1-9621c24d3178`, 525 s; attempt record sha256 `98ed6279b85c3f43c2737214f575d0592ec39dd45e710979f1f57542ced2092e`, stdout sha256 `a384334139a1049f83ccdf8ab15456e6fd82edca666a022a1048f6c03235491d`.

Round 10's witness was right about a mount over the path but wrong about the ordinary life of cgroups: systemd removes an empty scope and may make one of the same name again, and a cgroup may be made again at an attempt's path. Within one boot and one set of namespaces, cgroupfs gives no inode number twice and cgroup v2 has no rename, so another directory of the same file system at a recorded path means the recorded one was removed, and a removed cgroup was empty. The other direction was settled by the project owner: Claude showed (-1051) that the namespace record and the witness can both be faked by a process holding CAP_SYS_ADMIN in a namespace of its own (fake `/proc/self/ns` links, a file bound over `cgroup.events`). Asked on 2026-11-09, the owner accepted, and had stated (CR-013, REQ-043 decision 3), that a same-uid process tampering with the host itself (its database and files, its launch, its namespace view) is part of the residual, with -554's process that evades the best-effort scan; the two checks stay as hardening against accidental changes.

| ID | Severity | Found by | Summary | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M2T06R-1000 | high | Codex | SIGTERM read `cgroup.procs` by path: in a cgroup made again at the attempt's path, an unrelated process was signalled (reproduced with the product host: CancelRun, the process ended by SIGTERM). | SIGTERM opens the attempt's cgroup and those below it one from another through the checked directory (891bdde). Test on real cgroups in a delegated scope: a process in a cgroup made again at the path gets no SIGTERM (red: signalled). |
| M2T06R-1001 | high | Codex | An ancestor scope made again by systemd under the same name made the old attempt's cgroup unprovable for ever: the attempt stayed RUNNING with the writer lease (reproduced with the product host). | Another directory of the same file system at a recorded path is its successor: the recorded one was removed (empty), so the attempt's cgroup is gone and the successor is neither read nor signalled; a directory of another file system still proves nothing (891bdde). Tests: a successor, populated, at the path or at its parent's, is gone and not read; another file system proves nothing; on real cgroups, both cases proved gone (red: held). |

Both reviewers found, independently, the same two defects of round 11's fix, and -1100 is the -1000 class again: -1000 moved SIGTERM onto the checked directory, while the removal after a proof still walked the path and so removed a successor's empty cgroups. Design correction (ADR-021 8d v7): after its record, every access to an attempt's cgroup starts from its checked directory (events, `cgroup.kill`, the members, the removal); the path is only compared with `/proc/<pid>/cgroup`; a cgroup proved gone (removed, a successor at its path, another boot) is not cleaned up at all. The SIGTERM walk and the removal now share one walk that holds a few descriptors however large the subtree (-1101).

## M2-T07 Team configuration, role bindings and role_epoch (PR #34, branch `task/M2-T07-team-config`)

The design is docs/adr/ADR-022.md (from a read-only Plan helper's draft, verified against main `d5ae06a`). The task is reviewed in two halves: slices S1 to S4 (configuration loader, StartRun's binding, ConfigureTeam, the run's checkpoint), then S5 to S7 (adapters' runner mode, `hoh __gate`, the product executor, TEST-018-02/03/05 and TEST-027-03 in the host).

### Round 1 (S1 to S4; two fresh sessions of two agents)

Scope: the diff `d5ae06a..1b2ca1e`; S5 to S7 out of scope. Codex focus the store and the API (ids M2T07R-01..49), Claude focus requirements, tests and design (-50..99). Codex FAIL; Claude FAIL.

Both reviewers found the checkpoint's first version reading a binding's availability as configuration, and Claude found that a role rebound with no session had its queued tasks blocked by the pace that follows in the same tick. Design correction (ADR-022 decision 8, second version): a role's change is judged only from the two configurations (the role in the revision its binding was made at and in the current one, its presence included); an unchanged role moves to the revision even with a live attempt; a changed role with none is applied only if the registry finds its new agent available, else the old binding and epoch stay and one team.binding_refused is written (REQ-019 decision 2); a binding without a session is not dispatched and its tasks are not judged by capabilities it does not have yet; every run not ended that is behind the project's revision is checkpointed each tick.

Test: a role blocked for BLOCKED_AUTH keeps state, reason, epoch and session when another role changes; removing an AGENT_UNAVAILABLE role is epoch+1 with an event. |
